# TCG Battle Pokémon Ability Support Audit

This generated set-by-set inventory covers 217 catalogued sets and 4537 Pokémon ability records with printed text. 3528 records match at least one implemented text family; 1009 do not match any family. Of those unmatched records, 3 are already guaranteed by a fixed battle-format rule; 486 require card zones or rules the engine does not model and have no direct gameplay equivalent; 520 remain in the implementation review queue. The skip categories use conservative text matching and need review when new kinds of effects appear. 17 additional ability records have blank source text and cannot yet be classified. Category counts are pattern matches and can overlap. “No recognized family” does not claim complete behavior coverage for records that match a supported family.

Recognized behavior families in the current simplified engine:

- holder damage modifier: 315 matching ability records across 127 sets
- team damage boost: 67 matching ability records across 47 sets
- team damage reduction: 52 matching ability records across 36 sets
- damage protection: 237 matching ability records across 111 sets
- Special Condition, sleep, and Energy-triggered status handling: 128 matching ability records across 65 sets
- status checkup damage boost: 19 matching ability records across 16 sets
- between-turn sleep check: 5 matching ability records across 5 sets
- Weakness rule: 47 matching ability records across 35 sets
- Ability type change: 57 matching ability records across 32 sets
- healing rules: 57 matching ability records across 42 sets
- healing amount boost: 38 matching ability records across 27 sets
- retreat rule: 201 matching ability records across 104 sets
- opponent attack cost increase: 19 matching ability records across 15 sets
- conditional attack cost reduction: 63 matching ability records across 35 sets
- end-of-turn draws and deck discards: 18 matching ability records across 7 sets
- activated healing Power: 130 matching ability records across 81 sets
- activated draw Power: 126 matching ability records across 67 sets
- activated information, opponent-hand interaction, deck shuffle, or temporary attack protection Power: 48 matching ability records across 31 sets
- deck search or Energy acceleration Ability: 416 matching ability records across 128 sets
- activated discard and draw Power: 54 matching ability records across 32 sets
- activated discard hand and draw Power: 27 matching ability records across 12 sets
- activated draw-to-hand-size Power: 58 matching ability records across 37 sets
- turn-start self-bench Ability: 5 matching ability records across 3 sets
- attack Knockout Lost Zone replacement: 5 matching ability records across 3 sets
- attack Knockout hand return: 5 matching ability records across 5 sets
- activated status Power: 66 matching ability records across 45 sets
- activated switch or devolution Power: 131 matching ability records across 69 sets
- first-turn evolution permission: 36 matching ability records across 22 sets
- attack or evolution action restriction: 72 matching ability records across 39 sets
- second attack per turn: 7 matching ability records across 3 sets
- Evolution and play-from-hand Ability effects: 256 matching ability records across 97 sets
- copied attack access: 38 matching ability records across 24 sets
- Pokémon swap Power: 120 matching ability records across 25 sets
- activated or triggered damage-counter Ability: 88 matching ability records across 52 sets
- activated damage or attack-boost Power: 9 matching ability records across 8 sets
- activated damage-counter moving Ability: 45 matching ability records across 39 sets
- damage-counter movement prevention: 1 matching ability records across 1 sets
- coin-flip damage prevention: 53 matching ability records across 36 sets
- attack effect prevention: 55 matching ability records across 42 sets
- attack damage and effect prevention: 61 matching ability records across 36 sets
- opposing Ability effect prevention (damage boosts and status): 20 matching ability records across 9 sets
- Active Pokémon effect bypass: 9 matching ability records across 6 sets
- conditional Ability suppression: 69 matching ability records across 43 sets
- Resistance rules: 14 matching ability records across 12 sets
- damage retaliation: 159 matching ability records across 84 sets
- between-turn damage effects: 31 matching ability records across 22 sets
- opponent Energy attachment damage: 24 matching ability records across 17 sets
- Bench-entry opponent deck discard: 3 matching ability records across 1 sets
- Bench Pokémon shuffle into deck: 8 matching ability records across 5 sets
- self-shuffle at low HP: 6 matching ability records across 5 sets
- discard-to-deck recovery: 8 matching ability records across 4 sets
- Energy attachment bonuses and Energy multiplier Abilities: 26 matching ability records across 16 sets
- opening-turn setup effect: 3 matching ability records across 3 sets

The Active Pokémon effect bypass applies only when this card attacks the opponent’s Active Pokémon; Weakness and Resistance still apply.

Damage rules cover holder and team damage boosts and reductions, fixed damage prevention and halving, coin-flip damage prevention, attacks whose non-damage effects are prevented, attacker-restricted damage/effect protection, supported attacks that ignore Resistance, damage/status retaliation after supported attack hits, and passive between-turn counter damage to selected targets. Opposing Ability protection currently blocks represented damage boosts and status conditions caused by activated Abilities. Energy attachment and multiplier effects include Ancient Trait α Growth, Harmonics, Venusaur's extra charge, and supported Energy-doubling Abilities; Energy doublers reduce effective attack cost when the modeled shared Energy type and attacker meet their type conditions. Other families cover Special Condition immunity and recovery, end-of-turn sleep checks, Poison/Burn checkup damage boosts, Weakness rules, passive healing and healing multipliers, passive retreat rules, passive attack-cost increases and supported conditional cost reductions, first-turn evolution permission, attack and evolution action restrictions, Team Rocket/Aqua/Magma counts, team size, named Bench Pokémon, and opposing Pokémon types, fixed end-of-turn card draws, once-per-turn Pokémon deck search into hand or onto the Bench, opponent-hand reveal, coin-triggered shuffling of both Pokémon decks, Pokémon swap Powers, and Active Pokémon healing, draw, hand-discard and draw, single-card discard and draw, draw-to-hand-size, Poison and Burn, and switching Powers. Other activated Poké-Powers, most Ancient Traits, energy attachment conditions, board-wide restrictions, and unmatched text still require individual review.

## Frequent unmatched families and their current dependencies

Basic Pokémon can now be played from hand onto the Bench during battle. Crobat V's Dark Asset draw-to-six and Dedenne-GX's Dedechange hand-discard-and-draw triggers are supported with their shared once-per-turn limits. Remaining Tapu Lele-GX and Lumineon V bench-play searches depend on Trainer cards in the battle deck; Prize-count effects need tracked Prize cards; Stadium- and Tool-dependent effects need those card zones and their lifecycle; Trainer-play triggers need Trainer cards and turn history; and Energy movement or attachment checks need individual Pokémon attachments. Adding those underlying battle-state systems would unlock multiple set families at once.

## All unmatched ability texts

These are exact printed-text groups with no recognized family, ordered by prevalence. “Skip” categories require mechanics with no comparable battle-engine representation; “Review” means the text may fit an existing mechanic or a reasonable approximation and should be considered for implementation. “Format-rule equivalent” means the printed restriction is already guaranteed by the battle format. Energy-card effects remain reviewable when they can be mapped to the shared Energy pool; this audit only skips effects that depend on Energy attached to a specific Pokémon or on card identity in a zone. Classifications are rule-based triage, not a substitute for card-by-card confirmation.
